```json
  "datasourceId": "a27821a6-6ccf-41ab-882e-9334ad3305b0",
  "limit": 100,
  "rationale": "I'm calculating the total revenue for each type of fee in fiscal 2025 by aggregating revenue from the fees dataset linked to the relevant fiscal period."
}
```

Output:
```json
{
  "columns": [
    "fee_type",
    "total_revenue"
  ],
  "rows": [
    {
      "fee_type": "ADVISORY",
      "total_revenue": 82686060
    },
    {
      "fee_type": "CUSTODY",
      "total_revenue": 8839504
    },
    {
      "fee_type": "PERFORMANCE",
      "total_revenue": 10522614
    },
    {
      "fee_type": "TRADING",
      "total_revenue": 693130
    }
  ],
  "correctedSql": "SELECT fee_type, SUM(amount_cents) AS total_revenue \nFROM api.finance.fees \nWHERE fiscal_period_id IN (SELECT id FROM api.finance.fiscal_periods WHERE fiscal_year = 2025) \nGROUP BY fee_type",
  "note": "original query failed and was auto-corrected — cite the corrected SQL"
}
```

#### Answer

In fiscal 2025, the wealth fee revenue for each type of fee was as follows:

- **Advisory Fee:** $82,686,060
- **Custody Fee:** $8,839,504
- **Performance Fee:** $10,522,614
- **Trading Fee:** $693,130

This total revenue was derived by aggregating the revenue amounts from the fees dataset for the corresponding fiscal periods in 2025. The analysis specifically accounted for the fiscal year and grouped the results by fee type. 

The corrected SQL query used was:
```sql
SELECT fee_type, SUM(amount_cents) AS total_revenue 
FROM api.finance.fees 
WHERE fiscal_period_id IN (SELECT id FROM api.finance.fiscal_periods WHERE fiscal_year = 2025) 
GROUP BY fee_type
```
This covers only the relevant fee types and excludes any unrelated data.

- **90 bps**: 4 portfolios
- **100 bps**: 12 portfolios

This indicates a range of advisory fees, with the most common being 100 basis points, paid by 12 portfolios. 

The analysis only includes portfolios that are currently open and paying a non-zero advisory fee.

```json
      "total_interest": 64159184,
      "total_payments": 165752523

      "total_interest": 7938006,
      "total_payments": 28077215
    }
  ]
}
```

#### Answer

For each loan product, the share of payments that went to interest is as follows:

- **AUTO**: \( \frac{9776511}{53799218} \approx 0.1819 \)
- **MORTGAGE**: \( \frac{64159184}{165752523} \approx 0.3870 \)
- **PERSONAL**: \( \frac{7938006}{28077215} \approx 0.2829 \)

### Summary of Findings
- **AUTO** products had approximately 18.19% of the payments going to interest.
- **MORTGAGE** products had the highest share, with about 38.70% of payments going to interest.
- **PERSONAL** loans had about 28.29% of payments allocated to interest.

These figures provide a clear view of how interest payments vary across different loan products.
